- Para OpenAI Codex / rutas subscription-included, el tracker **sí debe estimar costo API por tokens**, no marcarlo solo como included/0.
- Mantener el principio de no romper ejecución normal si falla la contabilidad.
- El problema real no era solo “estimación”: el tracker tenía un hueco de atribución por sesión vs por llamada.
- El usuario quiere la reconciliación correcta con Anthropic, y ahora también dejó claro que el sistema completo debe contemplar sesiones mixtas con OpenRouter Qwen/DeepSeek y otros proveedores.
- El tracker debe asumir sesiones mixtas de forma normal, no como excepción.

When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. {"success": true, "name": "hermes-agent", "file": "references/spend-monitor-ledger.md", "content": "# Hermes Spend Monitor / Native Cost Ledger\n\nUse this when the user asks to track Hermes model spend, token consumption, or cost by provider/model/project/channel.\n\n## Design lesson\n\nProvider dashboards are insufficient for Hermes because they usually answer “what did this API key spend?” but not “which Hermes project, platform channel, cron job, or session spent it?” Attribution must happen at the Hermes model-call boundary.\n\nPreferred architecture:\n1. **Local event ledger**: record one SQLite row per successful model response.\n2. **Use existing token/cost normalization**: Hermes already normalizes provider usage and estimates costs in `agent.usage_pricing`.\n3. **Provider reconciliation later**: OpenRouter/Anthropic/OpenAI reported totals can be pulled periodically, but local events remain the source of attribution.\n4. **Capture the canonical main-response path too**: do not rely on auxiliary-side instrumentation alone. The primary model response path must also write a best-effort spend event after `normalize_usage(...)` + `estimate_usage_cost(...)`, so OpenAI/Codex usage is not missed when it bypasses side-channel helpers.\n\n## Implementation pattern\n\n- Store the event ledger separately from session state, e.g. `$HERMES_HOME/spend.db`, to keep per-call accounting low-risk and independent from session schema migrations.\n- Add a module like `agent/spend_ledger.py` with:\n  - `SpendEvent` dataclass\n  - `record_spend_event(...)`\n  - `summarize_spend(days, group_by, limit)`\n  - `recent_events(limit)`\n  - helpers to infer `project_slug` from git root / workdir and build human channel labels.\n- Instrument the existing model response usage block in `run_agent.py` after `normalize_usage(...)` and `estimate_usage_cost(...)`, near where session token counters are incremented.\n- Make ledger writes best-effort: catch/log errors and never break chat or tool execution.\n\nUseful fields per row:\n- timestamp, provider, model, api_mode, base_url\n- session_id, parent_session_id, source/platform\n- chat_id, chat_name, chat_type, thread_id, gateway_session_key\n- channel_label, workdir, project_slug\n- input/output/cache/reasoning/prompt/total tokens\n- estimated_cost_usd, cost_status, cost_source, pricing_version\n- latency_ms, provider_request_id, raw_usage_json, metadata_json\n\n## CLI shape\n\nAdd a lightweight `hermes spend` CLI with group-by views:\n\n``​`bash\nhermes spend                    # default summary by provider\nhermes spend provider --days 7\nhermes spend model --days 7\nhermes spend project --days 7\nhermes spend channel --days 7\nhermes spend source --days 7\nhermes spend session --days 7\nhermes spend recent --limit 20\n``​`\n\nKeep output text-first and scan-friendly:\n\n``​`text\nHermes Spend — last 7 days\n\nTotal estimated: $18.42\nModel calls:     128\nTokens:          1,234,567 total · 900,000 in · 334,567 out\n\nBy project:\n- healthos: $8.75 · 41 calls · 420,000 tokens\n- daily-briefing: $4.10 · 12 calls · 190,000 tokens\n``​`\n\n## Telegram/channel naming preference\n\nFor DJ, never present Telegram topics as numeric labels like “topic 5” in user-facing spend reports or explanations. Use human-readable names when available. If Hermes only has numeric IDs, prefer the human group/chat label and say the exact topic name is unresolved; ask for/derive a mapping rather than exposing “topic N” as the label.\n\n## Tests and verification\n\nTargeted tests should cover:\n- recording two provider events and summarizing by provider/project/channel\n- recent event ordering\n- project slug inference from nested git-root workdirs\n- CLI rendering empty state and grouped totals\n\nIf `pytest` is not installed in the checkout, use repo tooling such as:\n\n``​`bash\nuv run --with pytest --with pytest-xdist pytest tests/agent/test_spend_ledger.py tests/hermes_cli/test_spend.py -q\n``​`\n\nAlso run syntax checks on touched modules:\n\n``​`bash\npython3 -m py_compile agent/spend_ledger.py hermes_cli/spend.py run_agent.py hermes_cli/main.py\n``​`\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- Do not infer spend after the fact from transcripts if you can instrument the model-call boundary.\n- Do not rely on provider dashboards for channel/project attribution.\n- Do not append numeric Telegram thread/topic IDs to user-facing channel labels for DJ.\n- Do not let accounting writes affect model calls; ledger persistence must be best-effort.\n- If the platform does not expose a true billed-cost feed, label billed spend as unavailable instead of `0` to avoid misleading reconciliation.\n- Subscription-included routes should still be costed with an estimated API-equivalent USD amount when the goal is visibility by model/workflow/session.\n- `argparse` parent/default values can leak into subcommands. For `hermes spend project`, prefer the subcommand name over a hidden parent `--by` default when deciding group-by dimension.\n", "file_type": ".md"}

### assistant

```text
The safeguard is: **we do *not* calibrate those models the way we calibrated Anthropic**.

What that means in practice:

- **Every model call is recorded per event**, not inferred from the final session row.
- **OpenAI Codex 5.4 / 5.5** are priced from token usage and kept as **`estimated`**, not `included/0`.
- **OpenRouter / Qwen / DeepSeek** are also priced from model metadata and kept as **`estimated`**.
- If a model’s pricing can’t be resolved, it becomes **`unknown`**, not silently `0`.
- **Only Anthropic currently has a calibration factor** because that’s the only provider we were able to anchor against a visible external total.

I verified the current state:

- `calibrated_providers = ['anthropic']`
- `openai-codex/gpt-5.5` → `estimated`
- `openai-codex/gpt-5.4-mini` → `estimated`
- `openrouter/qwen/qwen3-235b-a22b` → `estimated`

So the other models are **not** being handled by the same calibration path that caused the Anthropic mismatch.

The remaining protection is structural:
1. **per-call ledger**
2. **provider/model-specific pricing**
3. **no zero-fill when pricing is missing**
4. **no session-final-provider attribution**

If you want, I can add one more guardrail to the report so it always prints, per model:
- raw estimated cost
- whether calibration was applied
- pricing source
- `estimated / included / unknown`

That would make it much harder for any model bucket to be misread later.
```

{"mode":"replace","new_string":"def top_models(report: dict, max_rows: int = 4) -> list[str]:\n    rows = report.get(\"models\", []) or []\n    if not rows:\n        return [\"- No sessions found.\"]\n    lines = []\n    for row in rows[:max_rows]:\n        model = row.get(\"model\") or \"unknown\"\n        raw_cost = usd(row.get(\"raw_cost\", row.get(\"cost\")))\n        report_cost = usd(row.get(\"cost\"))\n        status = row.get(\"cost_status\") or \"unknown\"\n        calibration_factor = float(row.get(\"calibration_factor\") or 1.0)\n        calibration_text = \"none\" if calibration_factor == 1.0 else f\"x{calibration_factor:.4f}\"\n        pricing_sources = row.get(\"pricing_sources\") or []\n        pricing_versions = row.get(\"pricing_versions\") or []\n        pricing_source_text = \", \".join(pricing_sources) if pricing_sources else \"unknown\"\n        pricing_version_text = \", \".join(pricing_versions) if pricing_versions else \"unknown\"\n        lines.append(\n            f\"- {model}: raw {raw_cost} → report {report_cost} · calib {calibration_text} · source {pricing_source_text} · version {pricing_version_text} · {num(row.get('sessions'))} sessions · {num(row.get('total_tokens'))} tokens · {status}\"\n        )\n    return lines","old_string":"def top_models(report: dict, max_rows: int = 4) -> list[str]:\n    rows = report.get(\"models\", []) or []\n    if not rows:\n        return [\"- No sessions found.\"]\n    lines = []\n    for row in rows[:max_rows]:\n        model = row.get(\"model\") or \"unknown\"\n        cost = usd(row.get(\"cost\"))\n        status = row.get(\"cost_status\") or \"unknown\"\n        lines.append(\n            f\"- {model}: {cost} · {num(row.get('sessions'))} sessions · {num(row.get('total_tokens'))} tokens · {status}\"\n        )\n    return lines","path":"/opt/data/scripts/hermes_spend_briefing.py"}

```text
Honestly: **4/10** for you *as-is*.

Why:
- You prefer **current-state, scan-friendly, one-shot** reporting.
- This version is still a bit **too verbose** and mixes **raw/report/calibration/source/status** in one line.
- The diagnostic detail is good for debugging, but for your day-to-day you’d probably want the **important signal first**.

What *is* good:
- It makes the **raw vs reported** distinction explicit.
- It exposes **pricing source/version** so we can audit mistakes.
- It shows **status flags**, which helps catch silent misclassification.

What would make it more like **8–9/10** for you:
- Put the **headline totals first**
- Then a **very short top-model table/list**
- Then an **“audit details” section** only if needed
- Separate:
  - **raw estimated**
  - **calibrated reported**
  - **status**
  - **source/version**
- Avoid long inline text like `raw → report · calib · source · version ...` unless it’s in an audit block

My guess: you want the report to be **decisive at a glance**, and only expose the extra detail when it matters.
```

{"mode":"replace","new_string":"def top_models(report: dict, max_rows: int = 4) -> list[str]:\n    rows = report.get(\"models\", []) or []\n    if not rows:\n        return [\"- No model usage yet.\"]\n    lines = []\n    for row in rows[:max_rows]:\n        model = row.get(\"model\") or \"unknown\"\n        report_cost_value = float(row.get(\"cost\") or 0)\n        raw_cost_value = float(row.get(\"raw_cost\", row.get(\"cost\")) or 0)\n        report_cost = usd(report_cost_value)\n        raw_cost = usd(raw_cost_value)\n        status_flags = row.get(\"status_flags\") or []\n        status = \",\".join(status_flags) if status_flags else (row.get(\"cost_status\") or \"unknown\")\n        calibration_factor = float(row.get(\"calibration_factor\") or 1.0)\n        parts = [report_cost, f\"{num(row.get('total_tokens'))} tokens\", status]\n        if abs(raw_cost_value - report_cost_value) > 1e-9:\n            parts.insert(1, f\"raw {raw_cost}\")\n        if calibration_factor != 1.0:\n            parts.insert(2, f\"calib x{calibration_factor:.4f}\")\n        lines.append(f\"- {model}: \" + \" · \".join(parts))\n    return lines\n\n\ndef top_workstreams(report: dict, max_rows: int = 4, model_rows: int = 2) -> list[str]:\n    rows = report.get(\"workstreams\", []) or []\n    if not rows:\n        return [\"- No workstreams found.\"]\n    lines = []\n    for row in rows[:max_rows]:\n        label = row.get(\"workstream\") or \"unclassified\"\n        cost = usd(row.get(\"cost\"))\n        tokens = num(row.get(\"total_tokens\"))\n        parts = [cost, f\"{tokens} tokens\"]\n        models = row.get(\"top_models\", []) or []\n        if models:\n            model_bits = []\n            for m in models[:model_rows]:\n                model_bits.append(f\"{m.get('model') or 'unknown'} {usd(m.get('cost'))}\")\n            parts.append(\"models: \" + \", \".join(model_bits))\n        lines.append(f\"- {label}: \" + \" · \".join(parts))\n    return lines\n\n\ndef top_channels(report: dict, max_rows: int = 4) -> list[str]:\n    rows = report.get(\"channels\", []) or []\n    if not rows:\n        return [\"- No channel/topic labels captured yet.\"]\n    lines = []\n    for row in rows[:max_rows]:\n        label = row.get(\"channel\") or \"unlabeled\"\n        cost = usd(row.get(\"cost\"))\n        tokens = num(row.get(\"total_tokens\"))\n        lines.append(f\"- {label}: {cost} · {tokens} tokens\")\n    return lines\n\n\ndef should_emit(now_et: datetime) -> bool:\n    if os.getenv(\"HERMES_SPEND_REPORT_FORCE\") == \"1\":\n        return True\n    # Cron wakes at UTC candidates for 5 AM ET across DST.
